=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
A sequence X = (X_1, X_2, \ldots) of positive integers (possibly empty) is called a 1122 sequence if and only if it satisfies all of the following three conditions: (The definition of a 1122 sequence is the same as in Problem F.)

- \lvert X \rvert is even. Here, \lvert X \rvert denotes the length of X.
- For each integer i satisfying 1\leq i\leq \frac{|X|}{2}, X_{2i-1} and X_{2i} are equal.
- Each positive integer appears in X either not at all or exactly twice. That is, every positive integer contained in X appears exactly twice in X.

Given a sequence A = (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N) of length N consisting of positive integers, print the maximum length of a (contiguous) subarray of A that is a 1122 sequence.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the maximum length of a (contiguous) subarray of A that is a 1122 sequence.

Constraints


- 1\leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1\leq A_i \leq N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

8
2 3 1 1 2 2 1 1

Sample Output 1

4

For example, taking the subarray from the 3-rd to 6-th elements of A, we get (1, 1, 2, 2), which is a 1122 sequence of length 4.
There is no longer (contiguous) subarray that satisfies the conditions for a 1122 sequence, so the answer is 4.

Sample Input 2

3
1 2 2

Sample Output 2

2

Sample Input 3

1
1

Sample Output 3

0

Note that a sequence of length 0 also satisfies the conditions for a 1122 sequence.

- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Equivalent subarray can be partitioned into adjacent equal pairs, and each value appears exactly once among pairs (so no repeated pair value). Since contiguous subarray of A. So we need longest interval [l, r] (0-index inclusive/exclusive?) where length even, and for every odd offset within interval, adjacent elements equal. This means A[l]=A[l+1], A[l+2]=A[l+3], ..., A[r-2]=A[r-1]. Also pair values are distinct.

Let's define "good adjacent equal positions" maybe B_i = 1 if A_i == A_{i+1} for i=1..N-1. A subarray of even length 2m starting at l has pairs (l,l+1), (l+2,l+3), ... So it requires B_l = B_{l+2} = ... = B_{l+2m-2} = 1 (using 1-index positions). Additionally the values A_l, A_{l+2}, ..., A_{l+2m-2} are all distinct. Note if B_j=1 then pair value A_j=A_{j+1}. Need distinct values.

So problem reduces: choose start l such that B_l=1, and choose max m where positions l, l+2, ..., l+2(m-1) all have B=1 and A at these positions (or pair values) distinct. End r = l+2m. Need max 2m. Also length 0 answer at least 0.

Need compute maximum number of consecutive "good edges" at parity positions? Let's define for each parity p = l mod 2 (1-index). Need a run of good edges along indices l, l+2, l+4... all B=1. Since if B=1 at all positions in that run, pair values are A at those positions. Need maximum length of subarray of this run with all distinct values. Answer = 2 * max distinct count over all runs of B=1 on same parity.

Implementation:
import sys
def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N=int(input())
    A=list(map(int,input().split()))
    ans=0
    # freq as list of int length N+1 maybe faster
    freq=[0]*(N+1)
    # But using dict easier for clear. Since N 2e5, dict okay. Need O(N). Use dict.
    for p in (0,1):
        freq={}
        left=p
        # if p may be >= N-1? For N=1, range(1? p=1, N-1=0, range(1,0,2) empty; p=0 range(0,0,2) empty. left=p maybe 1, but no use.
        for i in range(p, N-1, 2):
            if A[i] == A[i+1]:
                v=A[i]
                freq[v]=freq.get(v,0)+1
                while freq[v] > 1:
                    u=A[left]
                    freq[u]-=1
                    if freq[u]==0:
                        del freq[u]
                    left += 2
                # after shrink, compute length
                cur = (i - left)//2 + 1
                if cur > ans: ans=cur
            else:
                freq.clear()
                left = i + 2
    print(ans*2)
if __name__=="__main__": main()

Need verify left when p=1 and N=2? range(1,1,2) empty, no issue. p=0 range(0,1,2) includes i=0. left=0. If bad, left=2. No next. Good.
If p=1 and N=3, range(1,2,2) includes i=1. left=1. If bad, left=3. No next. Good.
If p=0 and N=2, i=0. If good, add. cur=(0-0)//2+1=1. ans=2. Good.

Potential issue with dictionary freq and clear: Suppose we have freq with zero entries deleted. In while loop, if freq[v] >1, we remove left. But what if left edge value u equals v and after decrement freq[v] becomes 1, loop stops. Good. What if left edge value u not v, freq[u] decremented maybe to 0 and deleted. Continue. Good.

But wait: The while loop condition checks freq[v] > 1. If duplicate value v appears multiple times (e.g., three occurrences), removing one occurrence of v reduces count by 1. If there are two previous v's, after removing one, freq[v]=2 still >1, continue removing left. It may remove non-v edges until reaches another v. Eventually count becomes 1. This maintains all values distinct? Let's test values [1,2,1,1]. Add 1,2 len2; add 1 duplicate count2. Remove left 1 count1 stop; window [2,1] distinct. Add next 1: freq[1]=2, remove left 2 count0, left now at first 1? Actually window after previous was edges positions 2? Let's use list values [1,2,1,1]. After third, window [2,1]. Add fourth 1, freq[1]=2. Remove left value 2, freq[2]=0; left moves to value 1 (third). freq[1] still 2? Wait before adding fourth: freq[1]=1 (third), freq[2]=1. Add fourth: freq[1]=2. Remove left value 2 -> freq[2]=0. left moves to third value 1. freq[1] remains 2. Loop continues, remove left value 1 -> freq[1]=1. left moves to fourth. Stop. Window [1] (fourth). Distinct. Good.

But is it correct to shrink only until duplicate value count <=1, not necessarily until all duplicates removed? Yes because only duplicate is v; after count v=1, all values distinct? What about another value w with count >1? The invariant before adding was all distinct. Adding v can only make count of v >1; counts of other values remain 1. Removing elements from left decreases counts; cannot make other counts >1. So when v count <=1, all counts <=1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a need to handle input with line breaks? A may span multiple lines? Input format shows A_1 ... A_N on one line, but robust reading via `sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()` is better. Use:
import sys
data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
N=data[0]; A=data[1:]
This handles line breaks. We'll use that. Then code:
def main():
    import sys
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    ans = 0
    for p in (0,1):
        freq = {}
        left = p
        for i in range(p, N-1, 2):
            if A[i] == A[i+1]:
                v = A[i]
                freq[v] = freq.get(v, 0) + 1
                while freq[v] > 1:
                    u = A[left]
                    freq[u] -= 1
                    if freq[u] == 0:
                        del freq[u]
                    left += 2
                cur = (i - left)//2 + 1
                if cur > ans:
                    ans = cur
            else:
                freq.clear()
                left = i + 2
    print(ans*2)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

      left = i+2

This algorithm is more efficient and code shorter:
ans=0
for p in (0,1):
    last = {}
    left = p
    for i in range(p, N-1, 2):
        if A[i] == A[i+1]:
            v = A[i]
            if v in last and last[v] >= left:
                left = last[v] + 2
            last[v] = i
            cur = (i - left)//2 + 1
            if cur > ans: ans = cur
        else:
            left = i + 2
            # last not cleared; stale entries are < left
print(ans*2)

Let's formally prove last-occurrence algorithm:
For a fixed parity p, consider only edge indices i = p, p+2, ... where A[i]=A[i+1]. A valid window is a contiguous block of such indices (no bad edge) with distinct values. We scan good edges in order; bad edges reset `left` to the next edge index, effectively starting a new block. Within a block, the standard two-pointer/last-occurrence method maintains the longest valid window ending at current edge. `last[v]` stores the latest index of value v seen so far (including stale from previous blocks). If `last[v] >= left`, then v occurs in the current window, so to make room for current v, the window start must move to just after that occurrence. Then update `last[v]=i`. The current window length is number of edges from left to i inclusive: `(i-left)//2+1`. This is correct even with stale entries because any stale entry has index < left and fails the `>= left` test.

Need consider if `last[v]` might be an edge index from a previous block but still `>= left` due to left not reset? But left is always reset to after bad edge, which is greater than all previous indices. So no. If no bad edge, there is no previous block. Good.

Now, let's test with a case where left moves due to duplicate, then a bad edge occurs. Example values [1,2,1] then bad. After duplicate at i4, left=2. Bad at i6, reset left=8. last1=4, last2=2. Both <8. Good.

Now, what about if bad edge occurs at i, but left is already > i? We said impossible. Let's verify with last algorithm: left can move to last[v]+2, where last[v] <= current i-2 (previous edge), so left <= current i. At next iteration i increases by 2, so left <= i. If current edge bad, left <= i. Reset left=i+2. Good.

Now, let's consider if `last[v]` can be equal to current i? We update after duplicate check, so before update, last[v] is previous occurrence < i. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a value v that appears at current edge i and also at previous edge i-2; duplicate detected, left = i. Then cur = (i-i)//2+1=1. This means window of length1 (current edge) valid. But what about if there are other values after previous occurrence before current? They are dropped. Good.

Now, let's decide final code. I think list-based last-occurrence is clean:
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:]
    ans = 0
    for p in (0, 1):
        last = [-1] * (N + 1)
        left = p
        for i in range(p, N - 1, 2):
            if A[i] == A[i + 1]:
                v = A[i]
                if last[v] >= left:
                    left = last[v] + 2
                last[v] = i
                cur = (i - left) // 2 + 1
                if cur > ans:
                    ans = cur
            else:
                left = i + 2
    print(ans * 2)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the standard last-occurrence algorithm when there are duplicate values and left moves, but `last` entries for values before left are stale. We rely on `last[v] >= left` to determine if v is in current window. Is it possible that a value v is in current window but its last occurrence is before left? No, if it's in current window, its last occurrence is at some position >= left. If it appears multiple times in current window, that would violate distinctness; but before adding current edge, window is distinct, so each value in window has exactly one occurrence >= left. Thus last[v] >= left. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "last[v]" storing the latest occurrence of v even if that occurrence is before left but there is another occurrence of v after left? If there is another occurrence after left, then latest occurrence would be after left, not before. So no.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "All input values are integers" and no empty input; no need for `if not data`. But including it is safe.
Code:
def main():
    import sys
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    ans = 0
    for p in (0, 1):
        last = [-1] * (N + 1)
        left = p
        for i in range(p, N - 1, 2):
            if A[i] == A[i + 1]:
                v = A[i]
                if last[v] >= left:
                    left = last[v] + 2
                last[v] = i
                cur = (i - left) // 2 + 1
                if cur > ans:
                    ans = cur
            else:
                left = i + 2
    print(ans * 2)
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "left = p" and p
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1 + N]

    ans = 0

    # A subarray of even length uses edges with the same parity:
    # starting at l, pairs are (l, l+1), (l+2, l+3), ...
    for p in (0, 1):
        last = [-1] * (N + 1)
        left = p

        for i in range(p, N - 1, 2):
            if A[i] == A[i + 1]:
                v = A[i]

                # If v already appears in the current window, move left
                if last[v] >= left:
                    left = last[v] + 2

                last[v] = i

                cur = (i - left) // 2 + 1
                if cur > ans:
                    ans = cur
            else:
                # A bad edge cannot be included, so reset the window.
                left = i + 2

    print(ans * 2)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
